<a id="hwn-041"></a>
## Note 41 — first-pass English COMPLETE

**Section:** H preface `HWN`. **Qum source page:** [140](https://ablibrary.net/book_content/b/12170/140?lang=ar). **Reported voice:** Mīrzā Muḥammad ʿAlī. **Arabic:** Full digital searchable-text working transcription. **Photo or manuscript certified:** NO.

### Arabic working transcription

<div dir="rtl" lang="ar">

قوله « أو خصوص ملة الإسلام . . . » : اى : ملة الإسلام والإضافة بيانيه . اعلم : ان الطريقة المخصوصة الثابتة من النبي تسمّى من حيث الانقياد له « دينا » ومن حيث انّه يملى ( اى : ينشأ ، والاملاء بمعنى الانشاء ) ويبين للناس « ملة » ومن حيث يردها الواردون والمتعطشون إلى زلال نيل الكمال « شرعا » و « شريعة » والدين يضاف إلى اللّه تعالى وإلى النبي ( ص ) وإلى آحاد الأئمة عليهم السلام والملة إلى النبي ( ص ) وإلى الأئمة ( ع ) . كذا نقل عن المصنّف . وعن الراغب : « الملة هي الدين غير أنها لا تستعمل الا في جملة الشرائع دون آحادها ولا تضاف الا إلى النبي ( ص ) الذي تسند اليه نحو : « اتبعوا ملة إبراهيم حنيفا » ولا تكاد توجد مضافة إلى اللّه ولا إلى آحاد أمة النبي ( ص ) فلا يقال ملة اللّه ولا ملتى ولا ملة زيد كما يقال دين اللّه وديني ودين زيد » هذا . والحق انها تسند إلى اللّه تعالى أيضا كما ورد في دعاء مكارم الأخلاق عن سيد السّاجدين وزين العابدين عليه الصلاة والسلام : « واجعلني على ملتك أموت وأحيا » وفي وداع شهر رمضان : « اللهم انا نتوب إليك في يوم فطرنا الذي جعلته للمؤمنين عيدا وسرورا ولا هل ملتك مجمعا ومحتشدا » . قال بعض المحققين : « فإذا وقع ذلك في كلام المعصوم ( ع ) وهو منبع البلاغة والبراعة فتحقيق التفتازاني لا حقيقة له وكلام الراغب لا يرغب فيه » انتهى . فان قيل : ان التقدير جايز وباب التجوز واسع . قلنا : ان الأصل في الاستعمال الحقيقة والتقدير خلاف الأصل فتأمل . وسيجئ الفرق بين الإسلام والايمان في تفسير قول المصنف « من تقرير عقايد الإسلام » إن شاء اللّه تعالى .

</div>

### English working first-pass rendering

Concerning “or the religion of Islam specifically”: the genitive construction here is explanatory—the expression means “the milla which is Islam.”

Know that a particular way established by the Prophet is called dīn insofar as people submit to it. It is called milla insofar as it is dictated, instituted and expounded to people; and it is called sharʿ or sharīʿa insofar as those approaching it, thirsty for the pure water of perfection, enter upon it. The word dīn may be related to God, to the Prophet and, on the account being quoted, to individual Imams; milla is related to the Prophet and the Imams. This distinction is reported from the author.

Al-Rāghib, on the other hand, states that milla is religion but is ordinarily applied to the complex of religious prescriptions, not to a single prescription. He further maintains that it is linked to the prophet to whom a community's law is attributed, as in “Follow the creed of Abraham, inclining to truth,” but scarcely ever to God or to an ordinary member of the community. Thus one says “God's religion,” “my religion” or “Zayd's religion,” but not in normal usage “God's milla,” “my milla” or “Zayd's milla.”

The glossator replies that milla can also be ascribed to God. He adduces invocations from the supplications associated with Zayn al-ʿĀbidīn, including a prayer to live and die upon “Your milla” and an invocation concerning a gathering of those belonging to God's milla. Another investigator is quoted as saying that such usage in the speech of an infallible Imam invalidates the restrictive views attributed to al-Taftāzānī and al-Rāghib. The glossator adds that appealing to an implicit omitted word or figurative usage is possible, but literal usage is the initial presumption, while positing ellipsis departs from that presumption.

He concludes by promising a later distinction between islām and īmān in connection with the phrase “establishing the doctrines of Islam.” The evidential force of the supplications and the polemical inference are the commentator's own; neither is independently authenticated here.

### Editorial/source qualification

Separate lexical definitions, al-Rāghib's usage restriction and Shiʿi supplication counterexamples. Do not present the attribution of quoted prayers as source-verified.

<a id="hwn-042"></a>
## Note 42 — first-pass English COMPLETE

**Section:** H preface `HWN`. **Qum source page:** [140](https://ablibrary.net/book_content/b/12170/140?lang=ar). **Reported voice:** Mīrzā Muḥammad ʿAlī. **Arabic:** Full digital searchable-text working transcription. **Photo or manuscript certified:** NO.

### Arabic working transcription

<div dir="rtl" lang="ar">

قوله « والأول أولى » : اى : كون المراد نفس الامر عموما هو الأولى لأنه يحصل عليه البراعة الظاهرة بالنسبة إلى قسمي الكتاب اعني المنطق والكلام بخلاف ما إذا كان المراد به خصوص ملة الإسلام إذ لا يحصل البراعة الظاهرة ح الا بالنسبة إلى قسم واحد من قسمي الكتاب وهو الكلام . فان قلت : انما قيد البراعة بالظّاهرة وكأنه يحصل البراعة بالنسبة إلى القسمين على الثاني أيضا لكنها لا تكون ظاهرة . ؟ قلت : نعم فان المنطق من مقدمات الكلام لاحتياجه اليه في ترتيب الأقيسة وانتاج النتائج ولذا قدمه المصنف عليه ، هذا . ولا يذهب عليك ان المذكور في تلك الرسالة هو القسم الأول خاصة افرده المحشى بالشرح . ثم المراد من البراعة براعة الاستهلال . والبراعة مصدر برع الرجل إذا فاق اقرانه واترابه . والاستهلال مصدر استهل الصبى إذا صاح عند الولادة ، ثم استعير لأول كل شئ ، فبراعة الاستهلال بحسب المعنى اللغوي تفوق الأول ، وفي الاصطلاح كون الديباجة مناسبة للمقصود كان يذكر في ديباجة كتب النحو مثلا « الرفع » و « النّصب » و « الجر » وغير ذلك مما يبحث فيه عنه وهو في التحقيق سبب لتفوق الابتداء لكنه سمى باسم المسبب تنبيها على كماله في السببيّة .

</div>

### English working first-pass rendering

On “The first is preferable”: interpreting “the middle of the way” as the truth of reality in general is preferable, because it allows the opening to foreshadow both parts of the book—logic and theology. If one restricts the image to the religious way of Islam, the suitability of the opening is immediately apparent only for the theological part.

An objection asks why the commentator said that the foreshadowing is “apparent.” Might the narrower religious interpretation also suit both parts, though less visibly? I reply: yes. Logic is an introduction or instrument preparatory to theology, since theology needs it for ordering syllogisms and deriving conclusions. That is why the author placed logic first. Yet remember that the separately commented treatise at issue here contains the first division alone, the logic section.

What is intended by barāʿa is barāʿat al-istihlāl, the skilful foreshadowing of the intended subject in a preface. Lexically, barāʿa comes from someone surpassing his peers; istihlāl originally denotes the infant crying out on being born and subsequently refers to the beginning of a thing. Together the phrase literally suggests the excellence of a beginning. Technically it is the appropriateness of an introduction to the work's principal aim—for example, a grammar preface which naturally introduces terms such as nominative, accusative and genitive. Such suitability is properly the cause of an excellent opening; it receives the name of the resulting excellence to stress how effectively it produces that result.

### Editorial/source qualification

Points to two-level scope: logic in its own treatise and logic as propaedeutic to kalām. Avoid attributing them to the wrong historical speaker.

---

<a id="hwn-043"></a>
## Note 43 — first-pass English COMPLETE

**Section:** H preface `HWN`. **Qum source pages:** [140](https://ablibrary.net/book_content/b/12170/140?lang=ar) · [141](https://ablibrary.net/book_content/b/12170/141?lang=ar). **Reported voice:** Mīrzā Muḥammad ʿAlī. **Arabic:** Full digital searchable-text working transcription. **Photo or manuscript certified:** NO.

### Arabic working transcription

<div dir="rtl" lang="ar">

يعنى قوله « لنا » ، فإنهم ربما يطلقونه ( الظّرف ) ويريدون به الجار والمجرور تشبيها لهما به في عدم الاستقرار والاحتياج إلى المتعلّق . ولان كثيرا من المجرورات ظروف زمانية أو مكانية فاطلق الظرف على مجموع المجرورات تجوزا فعلى هذا فالمراد بالظّرف المجرور خاصة . ثم إنهم ربّما يطلقون الجار والمجرور ويريدونهما معا لكنهما إذا أطلقا معا يراد بالجار ، الجار ، وبالمجرور ، المجرور كما أن لفظي الفقير والمسكين كذلك وفي ذلك الغز بعضهم الفقير والمسكين كالجار والمجرور إذا افترقا اجتمعا وإذا اجتمعا افترقا .

</div>

### English working first-pass rendering

The expression at issue is “for us” (lanā). Grammarians sometimes call such a prepositional expression a ẓarf, an “adverbial,” using that word for the preposition with its governed noun because, like an adverbial, it lacks an independent syntactic position and requires attachment to a governing expression. Moreover many expressions in the genitive denote times or places; the name of the adverbial is therefore extended figuratively to prepositional expressions. On one interpretation the word ẓarf refers specifically to the noun governed by the preposition.

Sometimes they use “the preposition and its governed noun” together to mean the whole construction. When the two terms are explicitly distinguished, however, “preposition” means the governing particle and “governed” means the noun following it. It resembles the names faqīr and miskīn, which may be treated as covering a common class when used singly and as distinct subdivisions when used together. Hence the riddle: “The poor and the needy are like preposition and governed expression: when separated they meet, and when joined they differ.”

### Editorial/source qualification

Ends Q141 at (Muḥammad ʿAlī), before an unnumbered gloss beginning 'al-ẓarf immā mutaʿalliq bi-jaʿala' by ʿAbd al-Raḥīm; that unnumbered passage is NOT silently included here.

<a id="hwn-045"></a>
## Note 45 — first-pass English COMPLETE

**Section:** H preface `HWN`. **Qum source page:** [141](https://ablibrary.net/book_content/b/12170/141?lang=ar). **Reported voice:** Mīrzā Muḥammad ʿAlī. **Arabic:** Full digital searchable-text working transcription. **Photo or manuscript certified:** NO.

### Arabic working transcription

<div dir="rtl" lang="ar">

جواب عما يرد على التوجيه الثاني اعني : تعلق الظرف برفيق من أنه مضاف اليه ومعمول المضاف اليه لا يجوز ان يتقدم على المضاف على ما بين في النحو . وحاصل الجواب : ان المنع انما هو فيما لم يكن المعمول من الظروف واما إذا كان منها فلا يمنع لاتساعهم فيها ما لم يتسع في غيرها .

</div>

### English working first-pass rendering

This answers an objection to the second grammatical explanation, which attaches the adverbial expression to “companion” (rafīq). Because “companion” stands as a genitive complement, one might object that the dependent of a genitive complement cannot precede the noun to which that complement belongs; this is an established rule of syntax.

The answer is that the prohibition applies when the dependent is not an adverbial expression. Adverbials receive a syntactic latitude that is not granted to other dependents, so the proposed preposing is not forbidden in the same way.

### Editorial/source qualification

Cross-reference to the preceding unnumbered marginal discussion needs a source-locator link; the numbered note itself is complete in searchable text.

<a id="hwn-046"></a>
## Note 46 — first-pass English COMPLETE

**Section:** H preface `HWN`. **Qum source pages:** [141](https://ablibrary.net/book_content/b/12170/141?lang=ar) · [142](https://ablibrary.net/book_content/b/12170/142?lang=ar). **Reported voice:** Mīrzā Muḥammad ʿAlī. **Arabic:** Full digital searchable-text working transcription. **Photo or manuscript certified:** NO.

### Arabic working transcription

<div dir="rtl" lang="ar">

قوله : والأول أقرب لفظا : يعنى ان تعلقه بجعل أقرب من حيث اللفظ من تعلقه برفيق وابعد من حيث المعنى . اما الأول فلكونه سالما من شائبة تقديم معمول المضاف اليه على المضاف فإنه وان كان جائزا عند بعضهم في أمثال ما نحن فيه ، لكنّه خلاف الأصل فان مرتبة المعمول بعد مرتبة العامل ومن التزام الحذف والتفسير أيضا على ما ذكرناه فإنه أيضا خلاف الأصل بخلاف تعلقه برفيق فإنه لا بد وان يلتزم فيه بواحد منهما والا لما يكون صحيحا من أصله . واما الثاني فلما فيه من شائبة ان جعله تعالى معلل بالعباد وقد قالوا : ان افعال اللّه تعالى لا تعلل بالاغراض وان اشتملت على منافع وغايات لا تحصى بل بالحكم والمصالح . وهذا وان كان ضعيفا سخيفا كما ستعرف في أواخر الكتاب لكن الحمل على ما يصح عند الجميع أولى من الحمل على ما لا يصح عند البعض كما لا يخفى . وقيل : لأنه يصير المعنى ح هكذا : « الحمد للّه الذي جعل التوفيق خير رفيق لانتفاعنا » فان اللام كما ذكر المحشى للانتفاع ولا يخفى ما فيه من سوء الأدب بخلاف ما لو تعلق برفيق كما لا يخفى . وقد يعلل : بأنه يكون المعنى ح انه تعالى : « جعل لانتفاعنا التوفيق خير رفيق » ولا يعلم منه ان مرافقة التوفيق لهم أو لغيرهم . إذ يجوز ان يجعل التوفيق رفيقا لغيرهم من الأصدقاء والأحباء ويكونون منتفعين بذلك كما هو ظاهر . والمقصود انما هو الأول كما لا يخفى على من تامّل . وفيه انه وان كان مطلقا محتملا لكل من الامرين ، الا ان الأول هو المتبادر في مقام الحمد وأيضا لا يبعد ان نقول : التقدير « خير رفيق لنا » فحذف الظرف لدلالة المذكور ولرعاية السجع .

</div>

### English working first-pass rendering

On “The first is closer in wording”: attaching the prepositional expression to “made” (jaʿala) is closer in grammar than attaching it to “companion” (rafīq), but it is further away in sense.

It is closer grammatically because it avoids advancing the dependent of a genitive complement before the noun to which that complement belongs. Some permit such preposing in constructions like this, but it is against the basic order, since the dependent normally follows its governor. The alternative also requires supplying a suppressed expression and then interpreting it, another departure from the normal order. Attaching the expression to “companion,” however, necessarily entails one or another of these expedients if the wording is to be syntactically sound.

The first attachment is more remote in meaning because it risks making God's act of bestowal dependent on a purpose obtained from His servants. Some theologians insist that divine acts are not explained by purposes, although they encompass innumerable benefits, ends, wisdoms and goods. The present glossator calls that exclusion weak and unsatisfactory and promises to discuss it near the end of the book; nevertheless, an interpretation acceptable to all disputants is preferable to one rejected by some.

Another objection is that the first attachment would mean, “Praise belongs to God, who made divine enabling the best companion *for our benefit*.” Since the lām marks benefit, the wording might appear irreverent. A third explanation adds that the wording would not specify *whom* divine enabling accompanies: God might make it a companion to another friend or beloved, while we benefit indirectly from that person's possession of it, whereas the intended sense is that enabling accompanies us.

The reply is that, although the grammatical construction could encompass both possibilities, in a context of praising God the first and intended one is naturally understood. One can also take the underlying expression to be “the best companion *for us*,” with the prepositional complement omitted because its sense is already indicated and the omission preserves the rhymed cadence.

### Editorial/source qualification

Important theological contrast: the writer explicitly rejects a categorical ban on divine final causation yet gives it interpretive accommodation. Note crosses Q141–142 and precedes (47).

<a id="hwn-047"></a>
## Note 47 — first-pass English COMPLETE

**Section:** H preface `HWN`. **Qum source page:** [142](https://ablibrary.net/book_content/b/12170/142?lang=ar). **Reported voice:** Shaykh ʿAbd al-Raḥīm. **Arabic:** Full digital searchable-text working transcription. **Photo or manuscript certified:** NO.

### Arabic working transcription

<div dir="rtl" lang="ar">

قوله توجيه الأسباب نحو المطلوب الخير : لا يخفى ان معنى التوفيق جعل الأسباب موافقا للمسببات ولما كان هذا معنى عاما شاملا على جعل الأسباب الخير والشر موافقة للمسببات والعرف لا يستعمله في جعل أسباب الشّر ، فلذا خص المحشى ( ره ) بالخير .

</div>

### English working first-pass rendering

On the phrase “directing causes toward the desired good”: tawfīq, divine enabling or facilitation, means bringing causes into correspondence with their results. In itself that would be a general description applying equally to causes that lead to good and causes that lead to evil. But ordinary usage does not call the arrangement of causes for evil tawfīq. That is why the commentator specified “good” when giving the definition.

### Editorial/source qualification

A separate unnumbered paragraph immediately following (47), attributed to Muḥammad ʿAlī, distinguishes tawfīq, ʿiṣma and luṭf. It remains an UNNUMBERED attached gloss, not secretly counted as note 47.

### Separately attributed UNNUMBERED gloss — not a numbered note

**Source voice:** Mīrzā Muḥammad ʿAlī; NOT assigned an additional HWN number. Working render, no independent photo verification.

<div dir="rtl" lang="ar">

والفرق بينه وبين العصمة هو انه يقال : للّطف الذي يختار عنده المكلف ترك المعصية واللطف أعم منهما إذ هو ما يختار المكلف عنده فعل الطاعة أو ترك المعصية وبعبارة أخرى : هو ما يقرب من الطاعة ويبعد عن المعصية .

</div>

Its difference from ʿiṣma (protection from sin) is that the latter denotes a grace through which a morally responsible person chooses to refrain from disobedience. Luṭf (gracious assistance) is broader than both: it is that in the presence of which the responsible person chooses obedience or avoidance of disobedience, or, expressed another way, what makes obedience nearer and disobedience more remote.

## Note 48 — first-pass English COMPLETE

**Section:** H preface `HWN`. **Qum source page:** [142](https://ablibrary.net/book_content/b/12170/142?lang=ar). **Reported voice:** Mīrzā Muḥammad ʿAlī. **Arabic:** Full digital searchable-text working transcription. **Photo or manuscript certified:** NO.

### Arabic working transcription

<div dir="rtl" lang="ar">

ومنه قوله تعالى ( في سورة التوبة الآية 103 ) : « خُذْ مِنْ أَمْوالِهِمْ صَدَقَةً تُطَهِّرُهُمْ وَتُزَكِّيهِمْ بِها وَصَلِّ عَلَيْهِمْ إِنَّ صَلاتَكَ سَكَنٌ لَهُمْ وَاللَّهُ سَمِيعٌ عَلِيمٌ » اى : وادع لهم ان دعائك سكن وتثبيت لهم .

</div>

### English working first-pass rendering

An example is the Qurʾānic verse (9:103), “Take from their property a charity by which you cleanse and purify them, and pray for them; truly your prayer is a repose for them, and God is Hearing, Knowing.” In other words, “Pray on their behalf, for your supplication brings them repose and steadiness.”

### Editorial/source qualification

Q142 continues with a long separately introduced, UNNUMBERED quoted investigation by Shaykh ʿAbd al-Raḥīm into the meaning of ṣalāt; do not silently attribute it to note 48.

### Separately attributed UNNUMBERED gloss — not a numbered note

**Source voice:** Shaykh ʿAbd al-Raḥīm, expressly introduced quotation; NOT assigned an additional HWN number. Working render, no independent photo verification.

<div dir="rtl" lang="ar">

قوله « هي بمعنى الدعاء » : قيل هي في اللّه تعالى الرحمة ومن الملائكة الاستغفار ومن الآدميّين التّضرع يجمعها قوله تعالى : « إِنَّ اللَّهَ وَمَلائِكَتَهُ يُصَلُّونَ عَلَى النَّبِيِّ . . . » . فان قيل : لا يجوز عموم المشترك فكيف استعملت الصلاة في معنييه ؟ قلنا : لا نسلم انها استعملت في كلا معنييه معا ، فان تقدير الآية : ان اللّه يصلى وملائكته يصلون . وانما لم يتعرض لهذا ، لان ما اختاره هو الأقوى للزوم هذا المعنى الاشتراك ، والمجاز خير منه . فان قلت : لو كانت الصلاة بمعنى الدعاء لا يجوز تعديتها بعلى ، لان على يدل على الضرر . قلت : هي هنا مسند إلى اللّه فتجرد عن معنى الطلب فلا يكون بمعنى الدعاء ، على انا لو سلمنا انها مسندة إلى الملائكة أو إلى المؤمنين ، يمكن الجواب : بان القول بان الدعاء إذا تعدى بكلمة على فهو للضرر مبنى على الأغلب الأكثر . فان ادعيتم انها للتضرر ابدا بطريق الايجاب الكلى فذلك وان ادعيتم انها للتضرر بحسب الغالب فذلك لا يصح كبرى للشكل الأول . ولئن أغمضنا عن ذلك أيضا فيمكن الجواب بان التضرر في الصورة المذكورة مخصوص بصريح لفظ الدعاء فلا يتعدى إلى الصلاة وان كان المعنى واحدا .

</div>

On the statement that ṣalāt means supplication: some say it means mercy when predicated of God, a request for forgiveness when predicated of angels, and entreaty when predicated of human beings. These usages are gathered in the verse “God and His angels send ṣalāt upon the Prophet.” One may object that a homonymous term cannot be used in several separate senses at once. The response denies that the occurrence must be one indivisible predication: one may understand “God sends ṣalāt” and “the angels send ṣalāt” as distributed statements. Yet a single broader or figurative account may be preferable to forced homonymy. Another objection says that if ṣalāt means duʿāʾ, its use with ʿalā is inappropriate, since that particle commonly accompanies a prayer against someone. The reply distinguishes the divine case (where there is no needy request) and challenges the illicit conversion of a frequent usage into a universal rule. If adverse sense merely occurs *most* often with duʿāʾ ʿalā, it cannot serve as the universally affirmative major premise of a first-figure syllogism. Even the stronger objection can be restricted to the explicit word duʿāʾ, rather than carried over to ṣalāt despite their contextual similarity.

<a id="hwn-050"></a>
## Note 50 — first-pass English COMPLETE

**Section:** H preface `HWN`. **Qum source pages:** [142](https://ablibrary.net/book_content/b/12170/142?lang=ar) · [143](https://ablibrary.net/book_content/b/12170/143?lang=ar). **Reported voice:** Shaykh ʿAbd al-Raḥīm. **Arabic:** Full digital searchable-text working transcription. **Photo or manuscript certified:** NO.

### Arabic working transcription

<div dir="rtl" lang="ar">

قوله وإذا أسندت إلى اللّه تعالى تجرد عن معنى الطلب : يريد انه لا منافاة بين كون الصلاة بمعنى الدعاء وبين كونها مسندة إلى اللّه تعالى لأنها تجرد عن معنى الطلب ، لان الطلب يدلّ على الاحتياج واللّه سبحانه منزه عنه ويراد به الرحمة اطلاقا للكل على الجزء وهذا هو المراد من المجاز . فان قلت : الرحمة في الأصل التعطّف وهو رقة القلب وهي كيفية نفسانية يستحيل في حقه تعالى . قلت : المراد بالرحمة هنا غايتها وهي الانعام وبهذا يؤل ساير الكيفيات النّفسانية المنسوبة اليه تعالى . وهذا معنى قول أهل العرفان : « خذوا الغايات واتركوا المبادى » .

</div>

### English working first-pass rendering

Concerning the words “When [ṣalāt] is attributed to God, it is stripped of the meaning of a request”: the intention is to deny any conflict between interpreting ṣalāt as duʿāʾ and attributing ṣalāt to God. In the divine case the signification of a needy request is removed, because requesting implies need, from which God is exalted. The intended sense becomes mercy, by employing a word for the more inclusive meaning and intending one aspect of it. This is the figurative usage mentioned by the commentator.

If you object that mercy in its primary sense means tenderness, an affection involving softness of heart, and therefore a psychological quality inadmissible of God, the reply is that what is meant here is its result—bestowal of favour. Other psychological descriptions attributed to God are interpreted by referring to their appropriate ends in the same way. This is the meaning of the saying of the people of gnosis: “Take the ends, and leave aside the beginnings.”

### Editorial/source qualification

The quoted Sufi dictum is a report, not an independently established maxim. The latter clause is on Q143, before note 51.

<a id="hwn-051"></a>
## Note 51 — first-pass English COMPLETE

**Section:** H preface `HWN`. **Qum source page:** [143](https://ablibrary.net/book_content/b/12170/143?lang=ar). **Reported voice:** Shaykh ʿAbd al-Raḥīm. **Arabic:** Full digital searchable-text working transcription. **Photo or manuscript certified:** NO.

### Arabic working transcription

<div dir="rtl" lang="ar">

قوله تعظيما واجلالا : نكتة لمجرد عدم التصريح ، واما نكتة التعبير عن الحضرة المحمدية صلى اللّه عليه وآله بكلمة « من » الابهامية هي الإشارة إلى أنه الفرد الكامل لهذا الجنس . وكأنه اطلق العام وأراد به الخاص تنبيها على أن هذا الخاص هو الفرد الكامل بحيث لا يتبادر الذهن منه الا اليه وكيف لا يكون كذلك ؟ وهو السبب لوجود العالم ولتعظيمه امر الملائكة ان يسجدوا لادم ( ع ) كما يدل عليه الاخبار .

</div>

### English working first-pass rendering

On “out of veneration and magnification”: that explains merely why the proper name is not pronounced directly. There is another rhetorical purpose in referring to the Muhammadan presence by the indeterminate word man (“the one who”). It signals that he is the perfect individual of the class in question: a universal expression is uttered while a particular individual is intended, drawing attention to the perfection of this particular so that the mind spontaneously thinks only of him.

The writer adds that the Prophet was the cause of the world's existence and that the angels were commanded to prostrate to Adam in honour of him, as certain reports indicate. These metaphysical and transmitted claims belong to the cited gloss; they are not established independently by the word man or by the grammatical argument.

### Editorial/source qualification

Distinguishes rhetorical argument from unverified reports about cosmic causality.

<a id="hwn-052"></a>
## Note 52 — first-pass English COMPLETE

**Section:** H preface `HWN`. **Qum source page:** [143](https://ablibrary.net/book_content/b/12170/143?lang=ar). **Reported voice:** Mīrzā Muḥammad ʿAlī. **Arabic:** Full digital searchable-text working transcription. **Photo or manuscript certified:** NO.

### Arabic working transcription

<div dir="rtl" lang="ar">

لما كان لقائل ان يقول : إذا كان المقصود من عدم التصريح بالاسم ، التعظيم والتنبيه المذكور ، فلم خص هذه الصفة من بين سائر الصفات مع أنه انما يحصل بها أيضا كما لا يخفى ؟ ، تصدى إلى الجواب وقال : واختار هذه الصفة من بين سائر الصفات لكونها مستلزمة لها بجميعها كما هو ظاهر . وقوله مع ما فيه من التصريح بكونه مرسلا ، جواب آخر يعنى : ان في اختيار تلك الصفة مع ما ذكر تصريحا بكونه صلى اللّه عليه وآله مرسلا .

</div>

### English working first-pass rendering

One might object that, if the purpose of not stating the name expressly is veneration and the special indication just described, why select this particular attribute rather than any of the other attributes, when those could achieve the same purpose? The commentator answers that this attribute was chosen because it implies all the other attributes. His further expression “in addition to stating explicitly that he is sent” provides a second answer: selecting this designation also announces explicitly that the Prophet is a messenger.

### Editorial/source qualification

One question and two distinct replies; no expansion of argument into theological proof.

## Note 55 — first-pass English COMPLETE

**Section:** H preface `HWN`. **Qum source pages:** [143](https://ablibrary.net/book_content/b/12170/143?lang=ar) · [144](https://ablibrary.net/book_content/b/12170/144?lang=ar). **Reported voice:** Shaykh ʿAbd al-Raḥīm (final signature Q144). **Arabic:** Full digital searchable-text working transcription. **Photo or manuscript certified:** NO.

### Arabic working transcription

<div dir="rtl" lang="ar">

قوله فان مرتبة الرسالة فوق النبوة : يمكن ان يكون هذا الكلام علة لكل من الوجهين اللذين ذكره في وجه اختيار المصنف صفة الرسالة . اما كونه علة للوجه الأول فبان يقال : اختار من بين الصفات هذه ، لكونها مستلزمه لسائر الصفات الكمالية ، اما استلزامه صفة النبوة ، فلان الرسالة فوق مرتبة النبوة اى : مشتملة على النبوة مع الزيادة ، إذ المرسل هو النبي الذي ارسل اليه دين وكتاب بخلاف النبي فإنه أعم من أن يكون له دين وكتاب أو يكون تابعا لدين النبي السابق . واما استلزامها صفة غير النبوة كالشجاعة والعصمة فظاهر خصوصا في نبينا ( ص ) فان ذاته العالية مستجمعة بجميع الفضائل والكمالات ونعم ما قيل : « آنچه خوبان همه دارند تو تنها دارى » ولذا لم يلتفت اليه . واما كونه علة للوجه الثاني فبان يقال : اختار المصنف صفة الرسالة من بين الصفات ، لان فيها تصريحا بأنه ( ص ) مرسل بخلاف باقي الصفات . اما صفة النبوة فلان الرسالة فوق النبوة فلا تسلتزمها حتى يكون التصريح بها في قوة التصريح بالرسالة . واما صفة غير النبوة فظاهر أيضا لا يحتاج إلى البيان . ثم اعلم : ان اثبات الفرق بين الرسول والنبي وان كان حقا كما ذكره صاحب المدارك في قوله تعالى : « وَما أَرْسَلْنا مِنْ قَبْلِكَ مِنْ رَسُولٍ وَلا نَبِيٍّ » حيث قال : هذا دليل بيّن على ثبوت التغاير بينهما بخلاف ما يقول البعض من أنهما واحد الا ان الفرق بما يفهم من كلام المحشى بالحديث الذي رواه أبو ذر الغفاري وهي ان النبي مائة وأربعة وعشرون ألفا والرسول منهم ثلاثمائة وثلث عشر ونزل لهم مائة وأربعة كتاب . فالحق في التفرقة على ما روى عن المعصومين عليهم السلام ان يقال : ان الرسول من رأى الملك معاينة ويتكلم به بخلاف النّبى فان النبوة يتحقق بالالهام والرؤيا والسماع بالصوت أيضا .

</div>

### English working first-pass rendering

The words “the rank of messengerhood is higher than prophethood” may provide the reason for either of the two explanations previously offered for the author's choice of the attribute “messengerhood.”

As a reason for the first explanation, the attribute is chosen because it includes the other perfections. Messengerhood includes prophethood and adds something to it: on this account a messenger is a prophet to whom a law and scripture are entrusted, while a prophet may either bring such a law or follow the religious law of a previous prophet. Its inclusion of other excellences such as courage and protection from sin is, the writer says, even clearer in the case of the Prophet Muḥammad, whose person embodies all perfections. He approvingly cites a Persian saying to the effect: “What all the excellent possess between them, you alone possess.” For that reason the commentator did not dwell on those other perfections individually.

As a reason for the second explanation, the designation explicitly proclaims the Prophet's being sent. Calling him merely a prophet does not entail messengerhood, since the former rank is broader; nor do the other descriptions convey this particular status as directly.

The glossator then examines the distinction between rasūl and nabī. The commentator of al-Madārik, in discussing the Qurʾānic phrase “We sent before you no messenger and no prophet ...” (22:52), takes the co-ordination as clear evidence that the two are not identical, contrary to some views. But the precise boundary between them is disputed. A report attributed to Abū Dharr gives a total of 124,000 prophets, of whom 313 are messengers, and speaks of 104 revealed books. The glossator instead prefers an account reported from the Imams: a messenger directly sees and converses with the angel, whereas prophecy can also be realised through inspiration, dreams or hearing a voice. This is a source-specific theological preference, not an independently authenticated account of the distinctions or numbers.

### Editorial/source qualification

Q143–144 cross-page; citation 'al-Madārik' and disputed prophet/messenger statistics require independent hadith/reference control; Persian verse translated approximately.

<a id="hwn-056"></a>
## Note 56 — first-pass English COMPLETE

**Section:** H preface `HWN`. **Qum source page:** [144](https://ablibrary.net/book_content/b/12170/144?lang=ar). **Reported voice:** Jalāl al-Dīn (signed). **Arabic:** Full digital searchable-text working transcription. **Photo or manuscript certified:** NO.

### Arabic working transcription

<div dir="rtl" lang="ar">

قيل هو مصدر بمعنى اسم الفاعل والظاهر أنه اسم للحاصل بالمصدر اطلق عليه مبالغة وقوله : « به الاقتداء » مبنى للمفعول ، اى : بان يقتدى به ، قوله « به » متعلق بالاقتداء ولا يليق تعلقه بيليق فافهم .

</div>

### English working first-pass rendering

Some say that this word is a verbal noun used in the sense of an active participle. It seems more likely that it names what is brought about by the verbal-noun action and is applied here by way of intensification. The phrase “to follow him” (bihi al-iqtidāʾ) is to be understood passively: “that he be followed.” The prepositional phrase bihi belongs with iqtidāʾ, not with yalīq (“is fitting”). Understand the distinction.

### Editorial/source qualification

Source note is a grammatical locator; subject of passive construction depends on principal lemma. Speaker signed Jalāl al-Dīn, not al-Yazdī.

<a id="hwn-057"></a>
## Note 57 — first-pass English COMPLETE

**Section:** H preface `HWN`. **Qum source page:** [144](https://ablibrary.net/book_content/b/12170/144?lang=ar). **Reported voice:** al-Taqrīb, p.9, quoted. **Arabic:** Full digital searchable-text working transcription. **Photo or manuscript certified:** NO.

### Arabic working transcription

<div dir="rtl" lang="ar">

قوله وحينئذ : اى وحين إذ جعلناه مفعولا له لقوله : « ارسله » لزم ان يراد بالهدى ، هدى اللّه حتّى يكون المصدر المذكور فعلا منسوبا لفاعل الفعل الذي هو الارسال المعلل بهذا المصدر ، اى علة ارسال اللّه النبي هو إرادة اللّه ان يهدى به الناس ولا مانع من أن يراد بالهدى هدى النبي نفسه بمعنى : ارسل اللّه النبي لأجل ان يهدى الناس إلى اللّه .

</div>

### English working first-pass rendering

On “and in that event”: namely, when we make the verbal noun “guidance” an object expressing purpose with respect to “He sent him.” In that analysis it appears necessary that the guidance be God's guidance, so that the action denoted by the purposive verbal noun has the same agent as the action whose purpose it states. The meaning would be that God's reason for sending the Prophet was God's will to guide people through him. Yet one may also understand “guidance” as the Prophet's own act of guiding: God sent the Prophet so that the Prophet would guide people to God.

### Editorial/source qualification

The same-agent grammatical rule is being discussed, not treated as undisputed in all syntactic schools.

<a id="hwn-058"></a>
## Note 58 — first-pass English COMPLETE

**Section:** H preface `HWN`. **Qum source page:** [144](https://ablibrary.net/book_content/b/12170/144?lang=ar). **Reported voice:** Shaykh ʿAbd al-Raḥīm. **Arabic:** Full digital searchable-text working transcription. **Photo or manuscript certified:** NO.

### Arabic working transcription

<div dir="rtl" lang="ar">

قوله وح يراد بالهدى هدى اللّه : وذلك ، لأنه اشتهر بين النحاة : ان حذف اللام من المفعول له لا يجوز الا إذا كان فعلا لفاعل الفعل المعلل به اى : بالمفعول به هو اللّه تعالى فلا بد ان يكون المراد بالهداية هدايته حتى يكون فاعلهما متحدين وذهب بعضهم إلى أن الشرط اغلبى لا كلى وهو مرتضى الشيخ محتجا بقول أمير المؤمنين صلوات اللّه وسلامه عليه وآله في نهج البلاغة : « فأعطاه اللّه تعالى النظرة استحقاقا للسخطة واستتماما للبلية » فان فاعل الانظار هو اللّه تعالى وفاعل الاستحقاق هو إبليس لعنه اللّه . قال لا يجوز جعل « استحقاقا » حالا من الفعل لان « استتماما » ح حال من الفاعل ويمتنع عطف حال أحدهما على حال الاخر . قال صاحب البهجة : لا يجوز أيضا جعل الأول حالا والثاني مفعولا له لوجود العاطف . نعم يمكن جعله حالا من المفعول باسناد الاستتمام اليه مجازا للسببية أو تأويله بتقدير الإرادة كما قالوا في قوله تعالى : « يُرِيكُمُ الْبَرْقَ خَوْفاً وَطَمَعاً » اى : إرادة خوفكم وطمعكم .

</div>

### English working first-pass rendering

The phrase “then guidance must be God's guidance” follows from a familiar grammatical rule: when the lām is omitted from a purposive accusative (mafʿūl li-ajlih), the purpose expressed by that verbal noun should be an action of the same agent who performs the action explained. Since God is the agent of sending, the guidance would have to be His, not another's, for the two agents to coincide.

Others regard this as the predominant pattern rather than a universal condition. The shaykh is said to prefer that view, adducing an expression in Nahj al-Balāgha concerning God's granting Iblīs respite “for his deserving wrath and for the completion of the trial.” The agent of granting respite is God, whereas the one deserving wrath is Iblīs. It has been objected that “deserving wrath” cannot simply be reinterpreted as a circumstance of the action while the coordinated “completion” is a circumstance of the agent, since one cannot coordinate such unlike circumstantial relations.

The author of al-Bahja adds that the first expression should not be circumstantial and the second purposive, given the co-ordinating particle. One alternative is to treat it as an accusative circumstance of the object, with an extended causal attribution, or to supply an understood intention. A comparable explanation is offered for the Qurʾānic expression “He shows you lightning, causing fear and hope” (13:12): one may understand “with the intention of your fear and hope.”

### Editorial/source qualification

Reported Nahj al-Balāgha example and grammatical conclusion not independently checked against an edition; keep the source argument conditional.

## Note 59 — first-pass English COMPLETE

**Section:** H preface `HWN`. **Qum source page:** [144](https://ablibrary.net/book_content/b/12170/144?lang=ar). **Reported voice:** Shaykh ʿAbd al-Raḥīm. **Arabic:** Full digital searchable-text working transcription. **Photo or manuscript certified:** NO.

### Arabic working transcription

<div dir="rtl" lang="ar">

قوله أو حال عن الفاعل : اى فاعل ارسله ، وهو الضمير المستتر الراجع إلى اللّه تعالى . ( فيكون المعنى ارسل اللّه النبي حال كونه هاديا ) وقوله : بل عن المفعول به ، الضمير في « به » راجع إلى « ال » الموصولة في المفعول ، اى : الذي فعل به الفعل . وفي كلمة بل إشارة إلى كونه حالا من المفعول به هو الأنسب بقرينة قوله : « هو بالاهتداء حقيق ونورا به الاقتداء يليق » فإنهما مناسبان للمفعول كما لا يخفى لذوي العقول .

</div>

### English working first-pass rendering

Concerning “or a circumstantial qualifier of the agent”: this means the agent of “He sent him,” namely the implicit subject referring to God. The resulting sense would be that God sent the Prophet while God Himself was guiding. The ensuing correction, “rather, of the direct object,” refers to the person upon whom the sending acts. The pronoun in bihi relates to that object. The correction particle bal signals that taking the verbal noun as the circumstance of the object—the Prophet—is more suitable, since the subsequent phrases “he is worthy of being guided/followed” and “a light by whom following is fitting” concern that object, not God as sender.

### Editorial/source qualification

Technical parsing of ḥāl; source phrase 'هو بالاهتداء حقيق' may have passive/active ambiguities treated in note61.

<a id="hwn-060"></a>
## Note 60 — first-pass English COMPLETE

**Section:** H preface `HWN`. **Qum source pages:** [144](https://ablibrary.net/book_content/b/12170/144?lang=ar) · [145](https://ablibrary.net/book_content/b/12170/145?lang=ar). **Reported voice:** Mīrzā Muḥammad ʿAlī. **Arabic:** Full digital searchable-text working transcription. **Photo or manuscript certified:** NO.

### Arabic working transcription

<div dir="rtl" lang="ar">

قوله وح فالمصدر : يعنى على تقدير كون الهدى حالا سواء كان عن الفاعل أو المفعول فهو اما بمعنى اسم الفاعل أو الاطلاق على سبيل المبالغة ، بيان ذلك : انهم ذكروا انه لا يجوز كون اسم المعنى خبرا أو حالا أو صفة لاسم العين لان كلا منها يستدعى الاتحاد في الوجود مع ما هو له ولا شك في تغاير المعنى والعين فكل ما يتوهم في الظاهر أنه من هذا القبيل فمبنى على التجوز اما في الكلمة أو الاسناد . اما الأول فبأن يقال : المراد من المصدر هو اسم الفاعل أو المفعول حيث يقتضيه المقام مجازا واما الثاني فبان يقال إنه باق على حاله لكنه اسند إلى الفاعل أو المفعول على سبيل التجوز في الاسناد فح يحتمل ان يكون المراد بقوله : « نحو زيد عدل » بيان النظير لكلا الامرين وان كان الظهور في الأخير فقط . ثم إنه لا يخفى انه يمكن في أمثال ما نحن فيه توجيه ثالث وهو ان يكون من باب المجاز في الحذف بناء على تقدير المضاف اما قبل اسم العين أو قبل اسم المعنى . فقولنا : « زيد عدل » مثلا اما على تقدير حال زيد عدل : أو هو ذو عدل ، لكن الأول لا يجوز هنا ضرورة ان المرسل بالكسر هو نفسه تعالى لا حاله وشأنه وكذا المرسل بالفتح هو نفسه ( ص ) لا حاله وشأنه وكأنه لهذا لم يتوجه اليه المحشى فتأمل .

</div>

### English working first-pass rendering

On “then the verbal noun”: if hudā (“guidance”) functions as a circumstantial qualifier of either the sender or the person sent, it must either be understood as an active participle or be used in an intensified figurative predication.

The underlying rule is that a noun of meaning or quality cannot literally serve as predicate, circumstance, or adjective of a concrete individual: those relations imply identity of existence between the subject and what is asserted of it, whereas the abstract meaning and the concrete thing are distinct. Whenever a sentence seems to say this, figurative usage must therefore occur either in the word or in the act of predication. In the first case a verbal noun is understood figuratively as an active or passive participle as appropriate. In the second the noun retains its meaning, but is attributed to an agent or patient figuratively, as in “Zayd is justice.” The commentator's example might be meant to illustrate both, though more obviously the second.

A third possible explanation is ellipsis: a genitive noun is suppressed before either the concrete subject or the abstract predicate. “Zayd is justice” could mean “Zayd's state is justice” or “Zayd is a possessor of justice.” But in the present case the first construction is unsuitable: the sender is God Himself, not God's condition or state; likewise the person sent is the Prophet himself, not his condition or state. Perhaps this is why the principal commentator did not pursue that analysis.

### Editorial/source qualification

Source crosses from Q144 to Q145; preserves three ways to resolve apparent predication of abstraction to a person.

<a id="hwn-061"></a>
## Note 61 — first-pass English COMPLETE

**Section:** H preface `HWN`. **Qum source page:** [145](https://ablibrary.net/book_content/b/12170/145?lang=ar). **Reported voice:** Shaykh ʿAbd al-Raḥīm. **Arabic:** Full digital searchable-text working transcription. **Photo or manuscript certified:** NO.

### Arabic working transcription

<div dir="rtl" lang="ar">

قوله مصدر مبنى للمفعول : لأنه لو كان مبنيا للفاعل يكون بمعنى قبول الهداية فلا يكون مناسبا لمقام النعت بخلاف ما لو كان مبنيا للمفعول فإنه ح يكون بمعنى مهتدى به وهذا المعنى مناسب للمقام ، هذا إذا لم يقدر في الكلام جار ومجرور واما إذا قدر بان يكون التّقدير : « هو الاهتداء به حقيق » فيصح ان يكون مبنيا للفاعل مع مناسبته للمقام لان المراد من الهداية ح ليس هدايته ( ص ) بل هداية غيره من الأنام .

</div>

### English working first-pass rendering

On “a verbal noun built for the passive”: if understood actively, the expression would signify accepting guidance and thus would not suit its role as a description of the Prophet. Under the passive construction it would mean “one by whom guidance is received,” and that is appropriate to the praise. This is so when no additional prepositional phrase is assumed. But if one supplies a phrase, taking the underlying wording as “it is fitting to seek guidance through him,” an active verbal noun can also fit: the guidance then means other people's being guided, not the Prophet's own being guided.

### Editorial/source qualification

Voice is signed Shaykh ʿAbd al-Raḥīm. Avoid fixing a single voice of the abstract noun beyond the competing parsings.

## Note 62 — first-pass English COMPLETE

**Section:** H preface `HWN`. **Qum source page:** [145](https://ablibrary.net/book_content/b/12170/145?lang=ar). **Reported voice:** Shaykh ʿAbd al-Raḥīm. **Arabic:** Full digital searchable-text working transcription. **Photo or manuscript certified:** NO.

### Arabic working transcription

فيكون محلها من الاعراب النصب فان الجملة التابعة لمفرد ، محلها بحسبه . وانما سميت الجملة جملة ، لان الجملة كما في ( ق ) جماعة الشئ .

</div>

### English working first-pass rendering

Accordingly, the sentence occupies the accusative position in syntactic analysis, since a sentence which follows a single word assumes that word's grammatical position. The word jumla (“sentence”) is so named because jumla denotes a collection or combination of a thing, as noted in the cited lexicon.

<a id="hwn-063"></a>
## Note 63 — first-pass English COMPLETE

**Section:** H preface `HWN`. **Qum source page:** [145](https://ablibrary.net/book_content/b/12170/145?lang=ar). **Reported voice:** Mīrzā Muḥammad ʿAlī. **Arabic:** Full digital searchable-text working transcription. **Photo or manuscript certified:** NO.

### Arabic working transcription

<div dir="rtl" lang="ar">

اى الاستيناف البياني وهو ما كان جوابا عن سؤال مقدم اقتضته الجملة المتقدمة كما في قولنا « نعم الرجل زيد » على أحد القولين لكن صدر الاستيناف هنا محذوف بقرينة السؤال المقدر فكأنه قيل : « من هو ؟ » قيل : « هو زيد » فكذا فيما نحن فيه فإنه إذا قيل : « ارسله هدى » فكأنه قيل : « لم ارسله هدى ؟ » فأجاب بقوله « هو بالاهتداء حقيق » . ثم لا يخفى ان الاستيناف وان كان في الأصل فعل المتكلم اعني : فصله الجملة الثانية عن الأولى لكنها سميت الجملة الثانية أيضا في الاصطلاح استينافا كما سمّيت مستأنفة على سبيل التجوز وسيأتي نظير ذلك من المحشى في أواخر الكتاب .

</div>

### English working first-pass rendering

The intended type is an explanatory fresh sentence (istiʾnāf bayānī): one that answers a question which the previous sentence naturally evokes. One analysis of “Excellent is the man—Zayd!” supplies such a relation. Here the opening of the new clause is understood through an omitted question. It is as though somebody asked “Who is he?” and received the answer “He is Zayd.” Likewise, after “He sent him as guidance,” one may ask “Why was he sent as guidance?” and receive the explanation “He is worthy of being guided by.”

Although istiʾnāf originally describes the speaker's *act* of starting a new independent sentence after ending the former, by technical extension the second sentence itself is called an istiʾnāf or a mustaʾnafa. An analogous naming convention appears later in the main gloss.

### Editorial/source qualification

The dialogue is explanatory reconstruction of rhetoric, not a source quotation of a historical conversation.

<a id="hwn-064"></a>
## Note 64 — first-pass English COMPLETE

**Section:** H preface `HWN`. **Qum source page:** [145](https://ablibrary.net/book_content/b/12170/145?lang=ar). **Reported voice:** Shaykh ʿAbd al-Raḥīm. **Arabic:** Full digital searchable-text working transcription. **Photo or manuscript certified:** NO.

### Arabic working transcription

<div dir="rtl" lang="ar">

قوله وقس على هذا قوله نورا : اى كل ما يجرى في قوله : « هدى » والجملة التي بعده يجرى فيهما أيضا ، إذ يحتمل ان يكون حالين مترادفين أو متداخلين ويحتمل ان يكون الأول مفعولا له فيكون المراد به تنويره تعالى حتى يكونا فعلا لفاعل الفعل المعلل به وان يكون حالا عن الفاعل بل عن المفعول فيكون بمعنى النور كما في قوله تعالى : « اللَّهُ نُورُ السَّماواتِ وَالْأَرْضِ . . . » أو اطلق على ذي الحال مبالغة وان يكون الجملة التالية صفة له أو مستأنفة . ثم أصل معنى النور هو الكيفية الظاهرة بنفسها المظهرة لغيرها . وانما اختاره على الضياء مع أنه أقوى من النور ولذلك أضيف إلى الشمس في قوله تعالى : « جَعَلَ الشَّمْسَ ضِياءً وَالْقَمَرَ نُوراً » وأيضا الضياء ضوء ذاتي والنور ضوء عارضى كما صرح به بعضهم ، اقتداء بالكتاب العزيز حيث ذكر النور بعد الهدى في قوله تعالى : « هُدىً وَنُورٌ » ولمجيء استعماله في مواضع شتّى وقد يقال : ينبغي ان يكون النور أقوى على الاطلاق بقوله تعالى : « اللَّهُ نُورُ السَّماواتِ وَالْأَرْضِ » وأنت خبير بان ذلك انّما يتّجه إذا لم يكن « النّور » في الآية الكريمة بمعنى المنور وقد حمله أهل التفسير على ذلك ، هكذا قيل .

</div>

### English working first-pass rendering

Apply the same analysis to the word “light” (nūran) and the sentence that follows it. Everything proposed for “guidance” and its following explanation applies here: the two may be parallel or nested circumstantial expressions; the first may be an accusative of purpose, in which case one understands God's illumination and keeps the purpose and principal action under the same agent; or it may qualify the sender or, more appropriately, the person sent. The verbal noun “light” may then signify the source of illumination, as in the Qurʾānic expression “God is the Light of the heavens and the earth,” or it may be predicated of the subject by way of intensification. The following clause may be adjectival or an independent explanatory sentence.

Light is originally that perceptible quality which is apparent in itself and makes other things apparent. Why choose nūr rather than ḍiyāʾ, although the latter seems stronger and is used of the sun where nūr is used of the moon? Some distinguish ḍiyāʾ as intrinsic radiance and nūr as an acquired or reflected radiance. The choice follows Qurʾānic usage, where nūr often follows hudā, “guidance and light,” and is used in many places.

Someone might argue that nūr must be the stronger designation, since God is called the Light of the heavens and earth. The reply is that this inference works only if the divine verse is understood as attributing “light” in the very same primary sense. Exegetes have also interpreted it as “the One who illuminates.” The verbal choice therefore does not by itself establish a fixed ranking of the two words' metaphysical strengths.

### Editorial/source qualification

The text offers competing interpretations of Qurʾān 24:35; do not take the metaphysical inference to be universally accepted.

---

<a id="hwn-065"></a>
## Note 65 — first-pass English COMPLETE

**Section:** H preface `HWN`. **Qum source pages:** [145](https://ablibrary.net/book_content/b/12170/145?lang=ar) · [146](https://ablibrary.net/book_content/b/12170/146?lang=ar). **Reported voice:** Mīrzā Muḥammad ʿAlī. **Arabic:** Full digital searchable-text working transcription. **Photo or manuscript certified:** NO.

### Arabic working transcription

<div dir="rtl" lang="ar">

قوله متعلق بالاقتداء لا ب « يليق » : وذلك لأنه لو تعلق به لكان المعنى : ان اقتداءنا يليق به ، بمعنى انه يصير كمالا وجاها له ( ص ) بخلاف ما لو تعلق بالاقتداء فان المعنى ح : ان اقتداءنا به يليق بنا بمعنى أنه يكون شرفا وعزّة لنا وهذا معنى قوله ( ره ) : « فان اقتداءنا به انما يليق بنا لا به . . . » فان قلت : إذا قدر متعلقا بالاقتداء يكون قوله : « يليق » مطلقا يحتمل ان يكون معموله المحذوف كلمة « بنا » وكلمة « به » لو لم يرجّح ذلك بناء على أن تقدير المحذوف من جنس المذكور ، أولى ولا دلالة للعام على الخاص فلا يكون ما ذكر وجها لترجيح التعليق بالاقتداء كما هو ظاهر . قلت : مع أنه يكفى في مقام الترجيح ان مجىء المحذور على تقدير التعليق « بيليق » قطعي وعلى الأخير غير قطعي ، ان القرينة الخارجية والحالية تدلان على أن المقدر هو كلمة « بنا » لا « به » فيرجح هو عليه ويثبت المطلوب والمرام من غير كلام .

</div>

### English working first-pass rendering

On the statement that bihi belongs with iqtidāʾ, not with yalīq: if the prepositional expression modifies “is fitting,” the meaning would be that our following is fitting *for him*, as though our imitation conferred an excellence or honour upon the Prophet. But if it modifies “following,” the sense is that *following him* is fitting *for us* and brings honour and dignity to us. That is why the main commentator says our imitation befits us, not him.

An objection remains. If bihi belongs to “following,” yalīq now stands without an explicit complement, and the missing complement could be either binā (“for us”) or bihi (“for him”). Unless the former is preferred—perhaps by the principle that a suppressed expression is likely of the same kind as what is explicitly mentioned—the general wording does not single out the desired particular meaning. The proposed attachment therefore might not resolve the problem.

The answer is, first, that attaching bihi to yalīq certainly produces the unacceptable implication, while attaching it to iqtidāʾ does not necessarily do so. That difference already favours the latter reading. Moreover the circumstances and surrounding context indicate that the omitted complement should be binā, not bihi. The intended distinction can therefore be maintained.

### Editorial/source qualification

The sentence about 'bestowing excellence upon the Prophet' is a rhetorical argument about inappropriate implication, not claim that following is otherwise worthless.

<a id="hwn-066"></a>
## Note 66 — first-pass English COMPLETE

**Section:** H preface `HWN`. **Qum source page:** [146](https://ablibrary.net/book_content/b/12170/146?lang=ar). **Reported voice:** Shaykh ʿAbd al-Raḥīm. **Arabic:** Full digital searchable-text working transcription. **Photo or manuscript certified:** NO.

### Arabic working transcription

<div dir="rtl" lang="ar">

قوله تقديم الظرف لقصد الحصر : قد اشتهر كلام الناس في ان تقديم المعمول يفيد الاختصاص وقد يفهم كثير من الناس من الاختصاص الحصر وليس كذلك وانما الاختصاص شئ والحصر شئ آخر والفضلاء لم يذكرون ذلك الحصر وانما اعتبروا بالاختصاص . والفرق بينهما : ان الحصر نفى غير المذكور واثبات المذكور والاختصاص قصد الخارجي من جهة خصوصه من غير تعرض لنفى غيره قاله التقى الدين السبكي . وقوله الفضلاء لم يذكروا في ذلك لفظ الحصر ، يرده ما قاله المحشى في هذا المقام وغير واحد من ذوى الافهام وما ذكره من التفرقة بين الحصر والاختصاص خلاف المشهور ، فان المشهور انهما مترادفان ، ثم ادعاء الاختصاص في تقديم المعمول ليس كليا بل اغلبى فاعتراض ابن الحديد بقوله تعالى : « كُلًّا هَدَيْنا وَنُوحاً هَدَيْنا » ليس بشيء .

</div>

### English working first-pass rendering

On the proposition that advancing the adverbial expression is meant to express exclusivity: it has often been said that putting a dependent expression before its governor conveys specification (ikhtiṣāṣ). Many understand that specification as restriction or exclusivity (ḥaṣr); yet Taqī al-Dīn al-Subkī distinguishes them. Restriction affirms what is mentioned and denies other possibilities; specification directs attention to the particular thing mentioned without expressly excluding other possibilities. Subkī says that earlier scholars spoke of the latter rather than the former.

The glossator objects to both parts of this account. The main commentator here, as well as other learned writers, does expressly speak of restriction; moreover, ordinary technical usage often treats the two expressions as synonyms. He adds a further logical distinction: fronting a dependent expression is *usually* a sign of specification, not a universal and exceptionless condition. Thus an objection from Ibn Abī al-Ḥadīd citing the Qurʾānic sequence “Each We guided; and Noah We guided” does not refute the account. One counterexample to an alleged universal would matter, but not to a rule claimed only for predominant usage.

### Editorial/source qualification

Al-Subkī's contrast and the responding glossator's denial are distinct voices. The cited Qurʾānic expression should be checked against the complete verse before formal citation.

---

<a id="hwn-067"></a>
## Note 67 — first-pass English COMPLETE

**Section:** H preface `HWN`. **Qum source page:** [146](https://ablibrary.net/book_content/b/12170/146?lang=ar). **Reported voice:** Mīrzā Muḥammad ʿAlī. **Arabic:** Full digital searchable-text working transcription. **Photo or manuscript certified:** NO.

### Arabic working transcription

<div dir="rtl" lang="ar">

قوله واما الاقتداء بالأئمة عليهم السلام : كأنه دفع توهم نشأ من قوله : « وحينئذ تقديم الظرف لقصد الحصر » . وحاصل السؤال : ان الاقتداء بالأئمة الاثني عشر عليهم السلام أيضا يليق بنا كما لا يخفى فلا يصح حصر اللياقة في الاقتداء بالنبي ( ص ) . وحاصل الدفع : انا لا نسلّم المغايرة بينهما فان الاقتداء بالأئمة ( ع ) عين الاقتداء بالنبي ( ص ) لظهور انهم كانوا مظهرى شريعته ومبلغى احكامه على الناس فإذا اقتدى أحد بهم ( ع ) فكأنه اقتدى به ( ص ) فصح كون التقديم لإفادة الحصر . ولو سلم فنقول : انّ الحصر إضافي بالنسبة إلى ساير الأنبياء ( ع ) لا حقيقي بالنسبة إلى جميع الناس . بيان ذلك : ان الحصر على قسمين : حقيقي وإضافي فان تخصيص الشئ بالشيء اما ان يكون بحسب الحقيقة والواقع بان لا يتجاوزه إلى غيره أصلا فهو الحقيقي أو بحسب الإضافة والنسبة إلى شئ آخر بان لا يتجاوزه إلى غيره فهو الإضافي ، ففي ما نحن فيه اما ان يكون الحصر حقيقيا بالنسبة إلى جميع الناس بملاحظة ان الاقتداء بالأئمة ( ع ) هو الاقتداء به ( ص ) كما سبق واما ان يكون إضافيا بالنسبة إلى سائر الأنبياء فلا يرد ح اعتراض حتى يفتقر إلى الجواب .

</div>

### English working first-pass rendering

The reference to following the Imams seems to pre-empt an objection arising from the claim that placing the complement first conveys exclusivity. If following the twelve Imams is also fitting for us, how can what is fitting be restricted to following the Prophet alone?

The reply is that these are not genuinely different kinds of following. In this commentator's Imāmī framework the Imams manifest the Prophet's religious law and convey its rulings. To follow them is therefore, in that respect, to follow him, and exclusivity is preserved. Even if that identity is not granted, the proposed restriction can be understood as relative rather than absolute. A *real* restriction (ḥaṣr ḥaqīqī) excludes everything outside the named referent; a *relative* restriction (ḥaṣr iḍāfī) excludes alternatives only within the relevant comparison class. Here it may restrict the appropriate following of the Prophet rather than that of the other prophets, without denying a derivative form of following the Imams.

The writer concludes that either interpretation prevents the apparent contradiction. His premise about the twelve Imams is specifically his own theological position, not a neutral definition shared by the entire commentary tradition.

### Editorial/source qualification

Keep the named Shiʿi premise attributed. The text's division real/relative restriction is analytically useful outside this confessional context.

<a id="hwn-068"></a>
## Note 68 — first-pass English COMPLETE

**Section:** H preface `HWN`. **Qum source page:** [146](https://ablibrary.net/book_content/b/12170/146?lang=ar). **Reported voice:** Mīrzā Muḥammad ʿAlī. **Arabic:** Full digital searchable-text working transcription. **Photo or manuscript certified:** NO.

### Arabic working transcription

<div dir="rtl" lang="ar">

فقلبت الهاء همزة ثم أبدلت الهمزة ألفا . فان قلت : فهلّا قلبت الهاء ألفا ابتداء ؟ قلت : لأنه لم يجيء ذلك في موضع حتى يقاس ذلك عليه ، بخلاف قلبها همزة فإنه شايع كثير وكذا قلب الهمزة ألفا .

</div>

### English working first-pass rendering

The proposed derivation changes hāʾ into hamza, then changes hamza into alif. Why not turn hāʾ directly into alif? Because, the commentator argues, that immediate substitution is not attested in a form suitable for analogical extension, whereas hāʾ becoming hamza and hamza becoming alif are individually familiar phonological substitutions. The two-step explanation seeks to make the etymology regular by appealing to separately attested changes.

### Editorial/source qualification

Proposed traditional derivation of āl from ahl; not an independent historical-linguistic demonstration.

<a id="hwn-069"></a>
## Note 69 — first-pass English COMPLETE

**Section:** H preface `HWN`. **Qum source pages:** [146](https://ablibrary.net/book_content/b/12170/146?lang=ar) · [147](https://ablibrary.net/book_content/b/12170/147?lang=ar). **Reported voice:** Mīrzā Muḥammad ʿAlī. **Arabic:** Full digital searchable-text working transcription. **Photo or manuscript certified:** NO.

### Arabic working transcription

<div dir="rtl" lang="ar">

قوله بدليل تصغيره على أهيل : يعنى : ان « أهيل » يدل على أنه في الأصل أهل فان التصغير يرد الأشياء على أصولها . ولقائل ان يقول : انا لا نسلم ان اهيلا بالهاء تصغير « آل » حتى يكون دليلا عليه ، لم لا يجوز ان يكون تصغير أهل بالهاء ؟ وقد نقل عن الكسائي أنه قال : سمعت اعرابيا فصيحا يقول : « آل » و « اويل » و « أهل » و « أهيل » وهو نص في ان اهيلا تصغير أهل وان تصغير آل اويل بالواو فلا وجه ح للقول بان أصل آل أهل والارتكاب بالقلب مرتين فافهم .

</div>

### English working first-pass rendering

On the argument “as shown by its diminutive form uhayl”: the intention is that the diminutive reveals an original form ahl, since diminutive formation often restores a word to its original morphological shape.

But an opponent may deny that uhayl, containing hāʾ, is the diminutive of āl at all. Why could it not simply be the diminutive of ahl? Al-Kisāʾī reportedly heard a fluent Bedouin pronounce the contrasting pairs “āl and uwayl” and “ahl and uhayl.” If that testimony is accepted, uhayl is specifically the diminutive of ahl, whereas āl has uwayl with wāw. The supposed evidence for deriving āl from ahl therefore disappears, and there is no need to posit the two successive consonant changes defended in the preceding note.

### Editorial/source qualification

The source quotes al-Kisāʾī's linguistic report; the historical accuracy and transmission of that report remain unverified.

---

<a id="hwn-070"></a>
## Note 70 — first-pass English COMPLETE

**Section:** H preface `HWN`. **Qum source page:** [147](https://ablibrary.net/book_content/b/12170/147?lang=ar). **Reported voice:** Mīrzā Muḥammad ʿAlī. **Arabic:** Full digital searchable-text working transcription. **Photo or manuscript certified:** NO.

### Arabic working transcription

<div dir="rtl" lang="ar">

قوله خصّ استعماله في الاشراف : اى : في العقلاء الذين لهم خطر عظيم ، جمع شريف وهو فعيل من الشرف محركة بمعنى العلو والمكان العالي تشبيها للعلو المعنوي بالعلو المكاني وفي الحديث : إذا اتيكم شريف قوم فأكرموه ، سئل وما الشريف ؟ فقال : الشريف من كان له مال ، قلت فالحسيب ؟ قال : الذي يفعل الأفعال الحسنة بماله وغير ماله . هذا . والحاصل : ان الآل أخص من الأهل مطلقا من جهة ان الأهل يعم العقلاء وغيرهم ، يقال : أهل الرجل لما له وعياله ، والآل يخص العقلاء فقط وأيضا هو يعم من العقلاء من له خطر وغيره والآل لا يستعمل الا فيمن له خطر دنيويا أو مطلقا كآل فرعون وآل محمد ( ص ) ، قيل لما ارتكبوا في الآل التغيير اللّفظى بتغيير الهاء ارتكبوا التّخصيص الأول توقيا للملائمة بين اللفظ والمعنى ولما كان الهاء حرفا ثقيلا لكونه من أقصى الحلق تطرق إلى الكلمة - بسبب قلبها إلى الألف الذي هو حرف خفيف - نقص قوى فارتكبوا التخصيص الثاني جبرا لهذا النقص .

</div>

### English working first-pass rendering

On the claim that usage is confined to persons of distinction: ashrāf refers here to rational persons of recognised standing. Sharīf is derived from sharaf, elevation, as a metaphor transferring spatial height to honour or social rank. A reported saying enjoins honouring the distinguished member of a people, and another explanation contrasts social eminence associated with wealth with noble action carried out through one's wealth or otherwise.

The conclusion is that āl is more restricted than ahl. Ahl may refer to both persons and things associated with a person, including possessions and household, whereas āl is ordinarily used of rational beings. Among those persons, ahl can include the prominent and the ordinary, while āl is applied especially to people of substantial worldly or general status, as in the family or people of Pharaoh and of Muḥammad.

A speculative phonological explanation is then offered: once the word had undergone a formal transformation, its meaning was narrowed to preserve an appropriate correspondence between form and signification. Moreover, because hāʾ is a heavy guttural and alif is light, the substitution supposedly reduced its weight, which a further restriction of meaning compensated. This is a medieval interpretive conjecture, not a demonstrated historical law of linguistic change.

### Editorial/source qualification

Reported saying and conjectural etymology are separate from observable usage. Do not treat later embellishment as verified language history.

---

<a id="hwn-071"></a>
## Note 71 — first-pass English COMPLETE

**Section:** H preface `HWN`. **Qum source page:** [147](https://ablibrary.net/book_content/b/12170/147?lang=ar). **Reported voice:** Shaykh ʿAbd al-Raḥīm. **Arabic:** Full digital searchable-text working transcription. **Photo or manuscript certified:** NO.

### Arabic working transcription

<div dir="rtl" lang="ar">

قوله وآله عترته : الآل اسم جمع لا واحد له من لفظه والعترة بالكسر نسل الرجل ورهطه وعشيرته . ثم هذا المعنى الذي ذكره المحشى هو مذهب الإمامية وذهب النوري والأزهري إلى أنه بمعنى الاتباع .

</div>

### English working first-pass rendering

On the expression “and his family—his ʿitra”: āl is a collective noun without a singular of the same form. ʿItra, with kasra, denotes a man's progeny, kindred and close group. The interpretation of the family in the cited passage as progeny is that associated by this glossator with the Imāmī school; he reports that al-Nūrī and al-Azharī instead understand the expression as referring to followers.

The distinction is one between a family defined by lineage and a larger body defined by adherence, with disagreement about how far the original designation extends.

### Editorial/source qualification

A substantial UNNUMBERED quoted hadith from al-Ṣādiq through al-Ḥasan about the Twelve Imams immediately follows this short numbered note. It is kept separate and its isnād/contents are NOT verified.

### Separately attributed UNNUMBERED gloss — not a numbered note

**Source voice:** Unnumbered report, cited after note71 and signed Muḥammad ʿAlī; NOT assigned an additional HWN number. Working render, no independent photo verification.

<div dir="rtl" lang="ar">

في حديث الصادق ( ع ) عن آبائه عن الحسن بن علي ( ع ) قال سئل أمير المؤمنين ( ع ) عن معنى قول رسول اللّه ( ص ) : « انى مخلف فيكم الثقلين كتاب اللّه وعترتي » من العترة ؟ فقال ( ع ) : « انا والحسن والحسين والأئمة التسعة من ولد الحسين تاسعهم مهديهم وقائمهم لا يفارقون كتاب اللّه ولا يفارقهم حتّى يردوا على الرسول ( ص ) حوضه .

</div>

A report transmitted through al-Ṣādiq and his forebears from al-Ḥasan b. ʿAlī relates that the Commander of the Faithful was asked who is meant by the Prophet's “family” in the statement that he would leave among the community two weighty trusts, God's Book and his family. In this report ʿAlī answers: himself, al-Ḥasan, al-Ḥusayn, and nine descendants of al-Ḥusayn, whose ninth is their Mahdī and standing leader. It adds that they will not part from the Book nor the Book from them until they arrive at the Prophet's pool. This is an *attributed report*, not an independently assessed historical or canonical conclusion.

<a id="hwn-072"></a>
## Note 72 — first-pass English COMPLETE

**Section:** H preface `HWN`. **Qum source page:** [147](https://ablibrary.net/book_content/b/12170/147?lang=ar). **Reported voice:** Shaykh ʿAbd al-Raḥīm. **Arabic:** Full digital searchable-text working transcription. **Photo or manuscript certified:** NO.

### Arabic working transcription

<div dir="rtl" lang="ar">

قوله مع الأيمان : اى مع الاعتقاد وقبول الشريعة . وقيل هم الذين أدركوا صحبة النبي ( ص ) ورووا عنه ( ص ) أيضا . والمشهور بين العامة : ان الصحابة كل مسلم رأى الرسول ( ص ) وقيل : أو رآه الرسول ( ص ) ثم الظاهر أنه ( أصحاب ) جمع صحب بالكسر مخفّف صاحب كنمر ( بفتح النون وكسر الميم وبكسر النون وسكون الميم وبفتح النون وسكون الميم ) وانمار . وجمع صحب بالسكون ( اى سكون الحاء ) اسم جمع كنهر وانهار لا جمع صاحب . إذا المشهور ان فاعلا لا يجمع على افعال كما ذكره المصنف وجمع الصاحب صحب ( بسكون الحاء ) كراكب وركب وصحاب ( بكسر الصاد ) كجائع وجياع وصحبان ( بضم الصاد وسكون الحاء . وصحابة بالكسر والفتح واصاحيب جمع أصحاب ) كشاب وشيبان . اما ما قاله المصنّف في بعض تصانيفه انه جمع صاحب فلم يرد انه جمع لفظي بل أراد به انه جمع معنوي .

</div>

### English working first-pass rendering

“Together with faith” means together with inward conviction and acceptance of the revealed law. Another account defines the Prophet's Companions as those who experienced his company and also transmitted reports from him. The glossator contrasts this with what he describes as the predominant Sunni definition: every Muslim who saw the Messenger, according to a further formulation including a person seen by the Messenger.

The discussion then turns to morphology. Aṣḥāb may be explained through ṣaḥb as a reduced collective form related to ṣāḥib (“companion”), just as forms naming groups of animals display competing singulars and plurals. The commentator distinguishes ṣaḥb with a vowel from ṣaḥb with a sukun and questions whether one of them is properly the direct broken plural of ṣāḥib, since the regular pattern fāʿil is not generally gathered into afʿāl without qualification. Other attested forms include ṣaḥb, ṣiḥāb, ṣuḥbān, ṣaḥāba and aṣāḥīb, each requiring attention to its vocalisation and morphological structure.

Where an author says that aṣḥāb is the plural of ṣāḥib, the glossator suggests understanding this as a plural *in meaning* rather than necessarily as a single direct morphological derivation.

### Editorial/source qualification

The Qum digital text includes a parenthetical modern dictionary insertion 'al-Munjid' and expanded zoological plural forms. These are not counted as historical glossator's authorial prose; photo verification needed.

<a id="hwn-074"></a>
## Note 74 — first-pass English COMPLETE

**Section:** H preface `HWN`. **Qum source page:** [148](https://ablibrary.net/book_content/b/12170/148?lang=ar). **Reported voice:** Mīrzā Muḥammad ʿAlī. **Arabic:** Full digital searchable-text working transcription. **Photo or manuscript certified:** NO.

### Arabic working transcription

<div dir="rtl" lang="ar">

الظاهر أن غرضه من هذا التحقيق مجرد بيان الفرق بين الصدق والحق ليكون كلام المصنف عارية عن شائبة التكرار وصحة الإعادة في الجملة . ثم الظاهر أيضا ان غرضه من زيادة قوله : « والاعتقاد » الايماء إلى أن المطابقة وعدمها للاعتقاد والحكم أولا وبالذات وللخبر ثانيا وبالعرض من حيث كونه حاكيا ودالا . فتأمل .

</div>

### English working first-pass rendering

The apparent purpose of this investigation is simply to distinguish ṣidq (truthfulness or truth of an assertion) from ḥaqq (truth or reality), so that the author's words do not seem repetitious and his additional expression remains meaningful. The added words “and belief” indicate something further: correspondence or non-correspondence belongs primarily and directly to belief and judgment, while it belongs to an uttered report secondarily and derivatively, insofar as the report narrates or signifies a judgment.

### Editorial/source qualification

Historical theory of judgment/report priority; not automatically a commitment to every modern correspondence account.

## Note 75 — first-pass English COMPLETE

**Section:** H preface `HWN`. **Qum source page:** [148](https://ablibrary.net/book_content/b/12170/148?lang=ar). **Reported voice:** al-Taqrīb p.10, quoted. **Arabic:** Full digital searchable-text working transcription. **Photo or manuscript certified:** NO.

### Arabic working transcription

<div dir="rtl" lang="ar">

قوله فان المفاعلة من الطرفين : يعنى إذا صدق ان هذا طابق ذاك ، فذاك أيضا مطابق لهذا . فالخبر والاعتقاد من حيث إنهما مطابقان ( بالكسر ) للواقع يسميان صدقا وهما أيضا من حيث إن الواقع مطابق لهما يسميان حقا وقد يطلق الصدق والحق على المفهوم الانتزاعي اعني : المطابقية ( بالكسر ) والمطابقية ( بالفتح ) أيضا .

</div>

### English working first-pass rendering

On “mutuality belongs to both sides”: if it is true that one item corresponds to another, the other may also be said to correspond to the first. A report or belief is called ṣidq insofar as it *corresponds to* reality; it may be called ḥaqq when reality is considered as corresponding *to it*. Sometimes both words designate the corresponding thing, and sometimes they designate the abstract relation or property of correspondence itself.

The point is an exchange of *aspects* in describing one relation, not the assertion that the real-world state of affairs was brought into being by the verbal report.

### Editorial/source qualification

Distinguish the active and passive participial vocalisation of muṭābiq and muṭābaq; source quote not the main Yazdī author.

## Note 76 — first-pass English COMPLETE

**Section:** H preface `HWN`. **Qum source page:** [148](https://ablibrary.net/book_content/b/12170/148?lang=ar). **Reported voice:** Mīrzā Muḥammad ʿAlī. **Arabic:** Short locator lemma ONLY — historical Arabic not fully transcribed. **Photo or manuscript certified:** NO.

### Arabic locator lemma

<div dir="rtl" lang="ar">

قوله فمن حيث إنه مطابق

</div>

### English working first-pass rendering

Why reserve the name ṣidq for the first consideration and ḥaqq for the second? The first is comparatively straightforward: ṣidq originally means reporting something as it really is. A report may receive the name of the action or quality that it brings about. That usage fits the direction in which the report corresponds to external reality. As for the second consideration, the report is treated as something established or fixed, to which the reality is then considered as corresponding. The term ḥaqq denotes what is established or real, and therefore fits this second standpoint.

### Editorial/source qualification

Two descriptions of correspondence, not the claim that a verbal proposition causes external reality.

<a id="hwn-078"></a>
## Note 78 — first-pass English COMPLETE

**Section:** H preface `HWN`. **Qum source pages:** [148](https://ablibrary.net/book_content/b/12170/148?lang=ar) · [149](https://ablibrary.net/book_content/b/12170/149?lang=ar). **Reported voice:** Mīrzā Muḥammad ʿAlī. **Arabic:** Short locator lemma ONLY — historical Arabic not fully transcribed. **Photo or manuscript certified:** NO.

### Arabic locator lemma

<div dir="rtl" lang="ar">

قوله وقد يطلق الصدق والحق

</div>

### English working first-pass rendering

The observation that ṣidq and ḥaqq can also name the abstract property of correspondence answers an anticipated objection. Earlier, ṣidq was explained as a report which corresponds to the real state of affairs and falsehood as a report which does not. But a proposition is subsequently defined as a statement capable of truth or falsehood. If truth and falsehood there mean *true report* and *false report*, the explanation becomes “a proposition is a statement capable of being a corresponding report or a noncorresponding report.” Because “proposition” and “report” here are effectively coextensive, the definition appears to employ what is being defined in its own definition, producing circularity.

The response is that ṣidq can name not only a report which corresponds but also the *correspondence itself*, while falsehood can name the corresponding absence of correspondence. In defining the proposition, the latter meanings are intended, and circularity is avoided.

An objection remains: the same words now have several significations. Using an equivocal term without qualification in a definition is generally defective. It might be replied that the property of correspondence is the literal, verbal-noun sense of ṣidq and that applying it to a true report is a metaphor, as in “Zayd is justice”; when a literal meaning is available, one need not choose the figurative one.

Two difficulties attend that answer. First, ṣidq has already been described as the act of reporting something as it is—a quality of the speaker—so using it for an abstract relation of correspondence may itself be figurative. Second, even if calling a corresponding report “truth” originated as a metaphor, usage among scholars is now so frequent that this sense comes to mind without any special indicator. Such conventional prominence cannot simply be ignored. A more convincing resolution is that, although a figurative signification can become as familiar as a literal one, the fact that taking it here would make the definition defective is itself an indication that the other intended sense is operative.

### Editorial/source qualification

Substantive two-page objection–reply chain on circular definition and conventionalized metaphor. Arabic source still lemma-only.

The prepositional expression is a nonpredicative adjunct (ẓarf laghw), with the bāʾ indicating cause. Why did the writer say “through assent” rather than “through conception and assent”? The verb “they attained happiness” (saʿidū) has also been taken as a subtle play on the name Saʿd al-Dīn al-Taftāzānī. This last association is reported as an observation, not as the established author's conscious intention.

**Section:** H preface `HWN`. **Qum source page:** [149](https://ablibrary.net/book_content/b/12170/149?lang=ar). **Reported voice:** Shaykh ʿAbd al-Raḥīm. **Arabic:** Short locator lemma ONLY — historical Arabic not fully transcribed. **Photo or manuscript certified:** NO.

### Arabic locator lemma

<div dir="rtl" lang="ar">

قوله بلغوا أقصى مراتب الحق

</div>

### English working first-pass rendering

The expression “they reached the utmost ranks of truth” is justified by a familiar grammatical principle: a plural noun in a possessive construction can express comprehensiveness. Hence “ascending the stages of truth” may mean ascending *all* those stages. To have climbed all of them entails having reached the highest or final stage; otherwise only some had been traversed. Maʿārij is the plural of miʿraj, an ascent or means of reaching what is true.

### Editorial/source qualification

The inference depends on reading the construct plural as universal in this context, not on ascent language by itself.

## Note 81 — first-pass English COMPLETE

**Section:** H preface `HWN`. **Qum source page:** [149](https://ablibrary.net/book_content/b/12170/149?lang=ar). **Reported voice:** Mīrzā Muḥammad ʿAlī. **Arabic:** Short locator lemma ONLY — historical Arabic not fully transcribed. **Photo or manuscript certified:** NO.

### English working first-pass rendering

This sentence answers an implicit question. The original wording merely says that they climbed the ascents of truth; it does not explicitly state that they attained the very highest rank. Why should an explanation add what the words do not bear? The reply is that the plural noun in construct with its definite complement signifies all the ranks. Ascending *all* ranks necessarily includes ascending to the last. If the highest had not been reached, only some ranks, not all of them, would have been traversed.

### Editorial/source qualification

Distinguishes a claimed quantificational implication from an otherwise unsupported expansion of the text.

<a id="hwn-084"></a>
## Note 84 — first-pass English COMPLETE

**Section:** H preface `HWN`. **Qum source page:** [149](https://ablibrary.net/book_content/b/12170/149?lang=ar). **Reported voice:** Shaykh ʿAbd al-Raḥīm. **Arabic:** Short locator lemma ONLY — historical Arabic not fully transcribed. **Photo or manuscript certified:** NO.

### Arabic locator lemma

<div dir="rtl" lang="ar">

قوله أو مستقر

</div>

### English working first-pass rendering

The word mustaqarr is the passive participle of “settled within,” originally with a complement which is suppressed through the frequency of the expression, just as an expression originally meaning “shared in” may be abbreviated to “shared.” A stable or predicative adverbial (ẓarf mustaqarr) is one whose governing expression has been omitted, whether the omitted action is general or particular. An ordinary or laghw adverbial has its governing expression present. This is the main commentator's preferred distinction; hence he attaches the expression to an understood “being connected with,” which is not one of the verbs of the most general existence.

The four commonly cited general verbal concepts are being, existing, standing or remaining established, and occurring or obtaining, as represented in a Persian mnemonic verse. Some add settling and its derivatives. The others count as particular activities. A further distinction, attributed to a learned Yemenite grammarian, is that when no specific contextual indication is present, the omitted governor is reconstructed as general; where the context points to a particular action, that more informative particular verb should be understood.

### Editorial/source qualification

The Persian mnemonic and exact vocalisation need a second linguistic pass; the substantive grammatical division is rendered.
